It sits inside every range the league reads, so no other design's score moved: compared with the last commit before it,
in five of the versions, every one of the 23 others scores exactly as it did. Its half-point under Crossover is the change
of start and the week fewer on one shift time.

**The pack.**
- **Its files.** `level-crossing.json` and `level-crossing.meta.json` in `tooling/`; its duty table is Crossover's,
  `results/crossover-table.json`. `regenerate.mjs` lists it in `SUPPLIED`; `fresh.mjs` has its one-line description,
  which names no other design, Home Grown's family and 7 Nov 2026.
- **Its sheet** differs from Crossover's only in the weeks on one shift time, the change of start, the rota itself and
  the most hours in any seven days.
- **The summary** lists 24. At its 80% floor the 24th row put the § footnote and the page footer on a second sheet, so
  dense cells give up their last half-pixel above and below; margins of 5mm, hairline borders and a quarter-pixel of
  padding each fell short, measured. The type and the floor are as they were.
- **The shortlist sheet** keeps its three.
  - Page 2's lead now reads Crossover first, 0.4 points ahead of Level Crossing, which it prints from the league.
  - Level Crossing's row says what it has beside Crossover, and the claims assert it: each day's duties Crossover's, no
    two closing weeks side by side where Crossover has them, one week fewer on one shift time. It keeps and pays what
    Crossover does against the three.
  - The 30-day, four-weeks-off and no-55-hour lists name it, and the league claims put it second, with Common Ground third.
  - The row's words were cut to one line, and headings and bullets give up a pixel each, to keep page 2 clear of its footer.
- **The read-me** names it among the designs drawn after the shortlist and says what it has. Crossover's lead is 0.4 points.
- **Tooling.** `exact-rota.py` gains `GENTLE_STARTS=` (which starts the first term of `OBJ=gentle` counts, 06:20 and 15:45
  unless set) and `GENTLE_SHARED=0` (drops its third term, which buys variety with the change of start). The defaults leave
  Middle Ground's objective as it was. Middle Ground's own `FIX=` line no longer returns OPTIMAL, before this change as after:
  its grid has had the 22:30 Saturday office since 6 Nov 2026 and its duty table has not (*The Saturday ticket office to
  22:30*).

**Commands.** The first returns OPTIMAL in seconds on the same objective (CP-SAT can return either of two equal layouts), so
the grid is committed and the second checks it:

```
cd docs/links-26/tooling
SKELETON=crossover.json OBJ=gentle GENTLE_STARTS=15:45 GENTLE_SHARED=0 ONE_MIN=18 H55=3300 LV_BEST=30 BEST_LEN=30 LV_FOUR=14 LV_WORST=19 FF11_FIXED=10 ISO=6 HEAVY=2550 LIGHT=1530 REST=860 python3 exact-rota.py results/crossover-table.json /tmp/lc.json 300
FIX=level-crossing.json SKELETON=crossover.json OBJ=gentle GENTLE_STARTS=15:45 GENTLE_SHARED=0 ONE_MIN=18 H55=3300 LV_BEST=30 BEST_LEN=30 LV_FOUR=14 LV_WORST=19 FF11_FIXED=10 ISO=6 HEAVY=2550 LIGHT=1530 REST=860 python3 exact-rota.py results/crossover-table.json /tmp/fix.json 60
```

**Checks:**
- `regenerate.mjs --check`: 24 proposals, every fingerprint as pinned;
- `check.mjs`: everything agrees;

## Links 24 removed (7 Nov 2026)

**The ask** (owner, 7 Nov 2026): *"Would it cause any damage to lose the links 24 work from the repo now, that is no longer
of use?"*; told that it would if deleted wholesale and not if the few live files moved first, *"Do it, but carefully. Don't
break anything. Then bug and regression check that work"*.

**What went:** `docs/links-24/`, 235 tracked files and 34.7 MB — the README, the hand-zipped `links-24-proposals.zip`, the
rules and summary PDFs, `presentations/` (8), `proposals/` (33), `silva-lining/` (6) and `tooling/` (184). 227 were deleted
and 8 moved. It stays in the history: **`git checkout 924b9dbc -- docs/links-24`** restores it whole (924b9dbc is main at
v25.46, whose `docs/links-24` is the folder removed here, tree for tree).

**What was kept, and why.** Eight files were still read, and each was moved before anything was deleted. The grids and the
decks are the same git blobs as before; the three scripts differ only by comments (a header note in each, and one above
the 24-line `folderStats`) and, in the two modules, their import depths:

| Was in `docs/links-24/` | Now | Read by |
|---|---|---|
| `tooling/report-data.mjs`, `tooling/cover-placement.mjs` (the 24-line ones, not this folder's) | `tooling/reference-24/` | `league.mjs`, for its Familiar Nine (24) row |
| `proposals/Familiar-Nine-F9-24-K31s.json` | `tooling/reference-24/` | that row, and `leave.mjs --check`'s second case |
| `presentations/Familiar-Nine-for-colleagues.pptx`, `…-for-managers.pptx` | `tooling/reference-24/` | `second-nature-decks.py`, which builds the template every deck in the pack starts from |
| `tooling/default-baseline.mjs` | `tooling/default-baseline.mjs` | `.claude/rules/links-design.md`, which sends a reader to it to measure the default, and `docs/LINKS_DEC2026_PLAN.md`, which quotes what it printed at 24 lines |
| `proposals/Fifteen-Turns-FT-24-EXT.json`, `proposals/Weekday-Lates-WL-24-EXT.json` | `test-fixtures/links-designs/`, lower-cased as the fixtures there are | `links-compare.test.mjs` and `links-compare-analysis.test.mjs` |

The Familiar Nine row could not simply go: it sits inside the main league's range (`REF_IN_RANGE=1`), so every score
depends on it. Without it all 24 main-league scores move (Crossover's by +0.32) and so does the order. `links-compare.test.mjs`
now refuses a grid it has no fixture for, rather than looking for it in the old folder.

**Proved unchanged.** Every output that reads those files was recorded before the move and rebuilt after it, with the
folder gone from the disk:
- **the league**, in all ten readings this README quotes (main; the proposals setting the scale; without familiar times; run
  on the fixed rota; Sunday duties in familiar times; the six-factor league; the four old readings): JSON and printed tables
  byte for byte the same;
- **`leave.mjs --check`**: the same, both cases ok;
- **the deck template** (`second-nature-decks.py`): both decks the same as a build from the untouched tree, part by part — 87
  and 69 parts, the parts inside the managers' deck's embedded workbook included — but for that workbook's own "modified"
  time, which every build stamps;
- **the shortlist and summary sheets**, rebuilt: text the same as the committed PDFs, which were left as committed;
- **`default-baseline.mjs`**: the same output from its old place, in a copy of the untouched tree, as from its new one.

**One figure corrected on the way.** `.claude/rules/links-design.md` quoted the December default's week-to-week step as it was
on 3 Nov (typically 2h 13m, largest 8h 51m, 4 of 26 over 2h; the script reproduces those on that day's tree). Three changes
to `links-default-targets.js` on 5–6 Nov moved it, and the line was not re-measured: it is now typically 2h 37m, largest 8h 36m, 5 of
26 over, still 10 unmeasurable across the cover weeks. Not caused by the removal; found because that line named the script
being moved. **Since #1707, merged the same day, the rule file quotes no step figure for the default:** a pass over the
rule files took dated measurements out of them, since they go stale as this one had. It quotes today's link's step only
and sends a reader to `default-baseline.mjs` for the default, so the figures above are kept here and nowhere else.

**Elsewhere.** The pack's read-me no longer offers `links-24-proposals.zip`, and `RULES.md` no longer points into the folder
for the 24-line rules. ESLint, `payroll-anonymity.test.mjs` and `doc-parity.test.mjs` lost the folder from their patterns;
`hosting-ignore-parity.test.mjs` and `deploy-hosting.yml` mention it only in history ("later `docs/links-24/`, removed 7 Nov
2026"). The scripts here that came from that folder say so, with the restore command. The dated
sections above, and `docs/LINKS_DEC2026_PLAN.md`, name `../links-24/` paths as they were; the note at the top of each says so.

**For a check of a new code or name** (the sections above checked them "against every code in `links-24/`"): the 24-line
codes began AC, B2, BB, C17, CF, CFT, CS, EF, F9, FO, FR, FT, JE, P2, PC, PT, Q2, QT, RR, S4, SL, ST, TF, TM, TN, WL, WL2,
WL3, WL4 and WS, each followed by `-24-`; the eleven shipped sheets were All Clear, Anchored Lines, Clean Sweep, Evening Peak,
Familiar Nine, Fifteen Turns, Full Overhaul, Polished Clean, Right Away, Silva Lining and Weekday Lates, and the withdrawn
designs are named in `docs/LINKS_DEC2026_PLAN.md` → Proposals.

**Checks** (after the move, the folder gone):
- `regenerate.mjs --check`: 24 proposals, every fingerprint as pinned;
- `check.mjs`: everything agrees;
- `deck-check.mjs`: 216 rows agree;
- `newlink-data.mjs --check`: current;
- `pack.py`: 70 files; every entry the same as a build before the change but the read-me (one paragraph fewer, still ASCII),
  `RULES.md` (its provenance line) and the two sheets (the committed PDFs, where the earlier build had packed a rebuild);
- `npm run check`: lint and typecheck clean; 4,274 + 1 + 2,473 tests pass and 4 skip, the same counts as before the change.

**Regression check** (the owner: *"Then bug and regression check that work"*), from a fresh checkout of the commit, which
has no old folder and nothing installed:
- `npm run test:nodeps`, the lane CI runs with nothing installed: 6,789 of 6,794 tests pass and 5 skip, the two compare tests
  among them;
- the ten league readings and `leave.mjs --check`: byte for byte the recording made before the move;
- the README's whole deck chain (`second-nature-decks.py` to `deck-marks.py`, then `deck-check.mjs`): all eight shipped decks
  rebuilt the same as the committed ones, part by part, but for the managers' embedded workbook's own build time;

- each kept file taken away in turn: the league, the leave check, the deck template, both compare tests and
  `default-baseline.mjs` stop with an error naming what is missing — ten cases, none carries on without its file.

An independent review of the commit found nothing broken and six points of wording, all corrected (the size is 34.7 MB,
not the 33.1 MiB first written; `folderStats`'s one caller, `folderRank`, is itself called by nothing; the three scripts are
not unchanged; and `test-fixtures/links-designs/` now has its `FILE_INDEX.md` entry). It also found `regenerate.mjs` running

## Both Ways and Fine Tune deleted (7 Nov 2026)

**The ask** (owner, 7 Nov 2026), after asking which designs other than Balanced V5, V6 and V7 were weak on the league
tables (those three stay whatever they score: their author's roster and two variations of it): *"Please cut both ways and
fine tune from the links 26 pack. Do all the necessary work regarding that, then give me the links 26 zip"*.

**Why these two.** Of the 21 designs outside Balanced V5–V7, over the league's ten readings Both Ways had the lowest average
place (18.7 of 21) and Fine Tune the fourth lowest (17.6, after Open Return's 17.9 and Second Look's 17.7). Neither was alone
best at any of the league's twenty measures; each tied for best only where many designs do (every December rule met, the
weekends, the contracted days, and a few more). Both Ways was last on two readings and Fine Tune on two.

| Design | Code · fingerprint | What it was | Main league before | Files deleted |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Both Ways | `BW-26-T3` · `2cb2d145` | one of the three drawn on 26 Oct (*Today's link on 26 lines*): a 13:35 or 13:45 every weekday and a 16:00 closer; never shortlisted | 21st of 24, 81.9 | its sheet, import file and JSON in `proposals/`; `tooling/both-ways.json` + meta; `results/both-ways-table.json`, its duty table |
| Fine Tune | `FT-26-H1` · `702d7a15` | Second Gear's weeks reordered by an outside reviewer (their `SGR-26-C1`), 3 Oct | 17th of 24, 82.7 | its sheet, import file and JSON in `proposals/`; `tooling/fine-tune.json` + meta; `tooling/external/second-gear-refined/`, the reviewer's read-me, candidate, search source, recount and its result |

Their local renders in `tooling/` (ignored by git) went too. Sixteen tracked files in all.

- **The reviewer's second folder** (`tooling/external/roster-search-results-2/`): Even Keel's own record (`SGR-26-C2`, with

  tooling that mention them as history.

## The outside review of the 22-design pack: the fatigue headline qualified, and the other designs on a sheet of their own (7 Nov 2026)

An outside reviewer checked the pack sent after *Both Ways and Fine Tune deleted* and reported no numerical discrepancy:
- all 4,004 rota cells against the 22 import files, and all 1,144 printed weekly hours totals;
- the contract totals, the shortest fixed-duty rest, opening and closing staffing, and the least floor cover;
- their own recount of the staffing-fit scores, and the eight decks' text against their PDF exports.

Their comparison of Common Ground, Crossover and Level Crossing was re-measured here from the grids, and agrees on every
figure (Common Ground · Crossover · Level Crossing):
- **Leave:** 30 · 30 · 30 days off at best for 14 days' leave, and 20 · 19 · 19 at worst.
- **Early finishes:** 12 · 20 · 20 a year before 14:00.
- **Saturday closer:** 8h 10m · 8h 40m · 8h 40m.
- **Fit, weekday · Saturday:** 32.7 · 9.8 on Common Ground, 31.4 · 17.6 on the other two.
- **A cover week placed badly:** 57h 00m · 50h 30m · 50h 30m in seven days with 8-hour cover duties, and 60h 00m · 52h 00m ·
  52h 00m with their 8h 45m ones.
- **06:20 starts:** 48 a year on Common Ground, 58 on the other two.

They recommended four changes. Two were made on the owner's word ("Do the other work to the links pack apart from the
shortlist change"):
- **The fatigue headline is qualified.** "No fatigue warning even if a cover week falls badly" claimed more than was
  tested.
  - The descriptions under Open Return, Crossover and Level Crossing, and the read-me, now say "in any cover-week placement
    tested". Their three sheets were re-rendered, and only the descriptions changed.
  - On the sheet of the other designs (below), "no 55-hour week" carries a footnote saying what was tested: a cover week's
    four duties worked together, wherever they fall from Monday to Saturday, at 8 hours and again at 8h 45m, its Sunday
    worked as overtime. A claim holds the footnote to both readings.
- **The other designs have a sheet of their own.** Page 2 of the shortlist sheet held a table of the other nineteen, one
  line each, and it grew with every design added.
  - It is now `links-26-other-designs.pdf`, one page, written by `shortlist-sheet.mjs` so that the claims pinning each
    row stay beside it.
  - What each design has and what it pays sit in two columns, A to Z within the pack's two folders, each design with its
    code. Balanced V7's row names whose gains it shares, where it used to rely on sitting under their row.
  - Page 2 keeps why these three and says where the others are; its lists take the room the table left.
  - Mutations confirm both new claims: "no 55-hour week" put on Grand Tour, which has the risk, stops the build, and so
    does a design dropped from the sheet.
  - The pack has 67 files.

The other two wait on the shortlist decision, which is the owner's and S. Silva's: bring Crossover or Level Crossing into
it, and give whichever joins a pair of presentations, as the shortlisted designs have.

**What it would take to bring Balanced V6 or V7 up to the leaders** (owner's question, the same day). Balanced V7 is V6's
duties laid out in new weeks, and the layout alone took it from 56.3 to 77.2. It is 10.1 points behind Crossover on the
main league:
- **About 7 points are the layout:** 13 of 21 weeks on one shift time, against 19; one fatigue warning when a cover week
  falls badly; three mixed weeks; a 2h 33m change of start, against 1h 31m; and a run of 7, against 6.
- **About 3 are the duties:** mainly the weekday fit, 40.0 against 31.4, and fewer of today's shift times.

Another layout of its duties could reach about 85 at best. The rest needs the weekday duties changed, which makes them,
more or less, Crossover's: Crossover already has V6's handover from the 07:30–16:00s to the 15:45 closers, and its Sunday
duty for duty.

**Checks:** the shortlist's claims and both sheets' footer guard; `regenerate.mjs --check`, 22 proposals; `check.mjs`;
`deck-check.mjs`, 216 rows; `newlink-data.mjs --check`; `pack.py`, 67 files with the read-me ASCII; and `npm run check`.

## Balanced V8 and Balanced V9 — Balanced V7's weeks laid out again (7 Nov 2026)

*Balanced V9 below is as first drawn (`8c679ae2`). Later that night two of its Saturday lates were swapped: heaviest week
42h 30m, league 83.3, fingerprint `e2b7d945` ("Balanced V8 and V9 searched again", below).*

**The ask.** The owner asked what would bring Balanced V6 or V7 up to the leaders (*The outside review of the 22-design
pack*, above: about 7 of V7's 10.1 points behind Crossover were the layout, about 3 the duties), then *"Do the v7 week lay
out again"*. Two layouts came back, and the owner: *"Do A as Balanced V8 and B as Balanced V9"*.

**What was kept, by construction.** Balanced V7's duties, every one, which are Balanced V6's
(`tooling/results/balanced-v7-table.json`): the three 15:45 closers and the 15-minute handover from the three
07:30–16:00s, the 13:35, 13:45 and 14:20 finishes, the staffing and the contract. Only which week works which duty, and
which days are rest days, were chosen again: every rest day this time, where V7 kept Grand Tour's.

**Why not the leaders' rest days as they are.** On Short Openers' rest days, which Crossover and Level Crossing use, these
duties cannot be laid with a 14h 20m shortest rest, even with every other rule loosened. `exact-rota.py` with `OBJ=rest`
shows the one rest that cannot be cleared: whoever works the Sunday 09:00–18:00 is back at 07:30 on Monday, 13h 30m later.
That duty needs a week with the Monday off and no 06:20 on the Saturday before. There, the only early week with the Monday
off follows a 06:20 Saturday, because the two 08:00–16:30 Saturdays are needed in the weeks before cover weeks, where a
06:20 Saturday is not allowed. Freeing the early weeks' rest days cleared it. The whole model, every rest day free, then
climbed from that grid; from nothing it had found no first rota in ten minutes. The nine steps are in `exact-rota.py`'s
header, and `FIX=` checks both grids against their floors: feasible.

**Held throughout:**
- no 55-hour week however the cover weeks fall (`H55=3300`);
- a shortest rest of 14h 20m;
- 14 days' leave buying 30 days off;
- four weeks off for 14 days' leave or less;
- at most 6 single rest days in 26 weeks, no week over 42h 55m, and at most 10 shifts between two-day breaks.

Then the most weeks on one shift time, and the smallest change of start.

**A worst stretch of 20, with the 55-hour rule held.** V7's worst stretch for 14 days' leave is 20, and *Crossover* recorded
that 20 had not been found with the 55-hour rule held: the whole model found no layout in 20 minutes, and freeing weeks of
Short Openers' rest days was proved impossible. From a grid that already met the 55-hour rule at 19, the whole model found
20 in ten minutes (step 6). It is not a rule a sheet tests, so nothing else changes; Crossover's and Level Crossing's 19 stand
as they are.

**Against Balanced V7**, every figure computed from the cells (the sheets, `factors-like.mjs`, the league):

| | Balanced V7 | **Balanced V8** | **Balanced V9** |
|---|---|---|---|
| Code · fingerprint | `MB-26-V7` · `75e9e7f3` | **`MB-26-V8` · `9ac65459`** | **`MB-26-V9` · `8c679ae2`** |
| Duties, closers, handover, finishes, staffing, contract | — | the same | the same |
| 14 days' leave buys: best · average · worst | 30 · 23.4 · 20 | 30 · **23.5** · 20 | 30 · **23.5** · 20 |
| Leave for four weeks off | 14 | **13** | **13** |
| Avoidable fatigue warnings (if a cover week falls badly) | 0 (up to 1) | 0 (**0**) | 0 (**0**) |
| Most days in a row (if a cover week falls badly) | 5 (up to 7) | 5 (**up to 6**) | 5 (**up to 6**) |
| Full weekends off; single rest days a year | 7 in 26; 12 | 7 in 26; 12 | 7 in 26; 12 |
| Weeks on one shift time | 13 of 21 | **19 of 21** | **18 of 21** |
| Weeks mixing earlies and lates | 3 | **0** | **2** |
| Runs of early or late weeks (with the cover weeks beside them) | 3 (5) | 4 (6) | 3 (**4**) |
| Week-to-week change of start | 2h 33m | **1h 39m** | **1h 51m** |
| Heaviest week, Monday to Saturday | 42h 55m | **42h 30m** | **42h 40m** |
| Individual weeks, Monday to Saturday (with the Sunday) | 24h 00m–42h 55m (30h 15m–42h 55m) | 24h 00m–42h 30m (29h 00m–42h 30m) | 22h 15m–42h 40m (29h 00m–42h 40m) |
| 06:20 earlies in a row (FF15) | 3 | 4 | 4 |
| Most hours in seven days | 50.5 | 51.0 | 51.0 |
| Steps between shifts, backward · forward (FF17) | 7 · 20 | 12 · 12 | 12 · 12 |
| Main league, of 24 | 22nd, 76.7 | **10th, 84.7** | **16th, 83.1** |

No figure is a fatigue warning, on either. What each pays against V7 is in the last four rows above the league:
- a lightest week with its Sunday of 29h 00m, against 30h 15m (and on V9 a lightest Monday to Saturday of 22h 15m, against 24h 00m);
- four 06:20 earlies in a row, against three;
- at most 51.0 hours in seven days, against 50.5;
- as many backward steps between shifts as forward.

V8 also pays with longer runs of early or late weeks. On 6 Nov the owner had turned down V7's first all-one-kind layout
because its runs were 5 (7), where V6's were 3 (5); V9 is the layout that keeps them.

**The league.** The 7.9 points V8 gains on V7 are all layout: the weeks on one shift time (2.3 points), the 55-hour week
gone (2.0), the mixed weeks (1.2), the change of start (0.8), the run (0.7), four weeks off for 13 (0.5) and the heaviest
week (0.4); V9 gains 6.4. On the league's layout measures V8 is now about 0.9 points ahead of Crossover. It is 2.1 behind
overall because of the duties, which cost it 3.0: the weekday fit to the trains (2.0), fewer of today's shift times (0.7),
and the long Saturday earlies and closers (0.3).

**Four weeks for 13 moved every other score.** Both new designs need 13 days' leave for four weeks off, where every other
design needs 14 or 15, so the league's range for that figure widened. Every design needing 14 lost 0.46 points, and those
needing 15 nothing:

|---|---|---|
| Crossover | 1st, 87.3 | 1st, 86.8 |
| Level Crossing | 2nd, 86.8 | 2nd, 86.4 |
| Common Ground | 3rd, 86.6 | 3rd, 86.2 |
| Next Step · Even Step · Fair Share | 4th–6th, 86.1 · 86.0 · 85.9 | the same |
| Closing Time · Grand Tour | 8th, 85.8 · 7th, 85.9 | **7th**, 85.8 · **8th**, 85.4 |
| Middle Ground | 9th, 85.8 | 9th, 85.3 |
| **Balanced V8** | — | **10th, 84.7** |
| Clean Slate · Short Openers | 10th, 84.6 · 11th, 84.5 | 11th, 84.6 · 12th, 84.0 |
| Home Grown · Second Edition · Even Keel | 12th–14th | 13th–15th, scores unchanged |
| **Balanced V9** | — | **16th, 83.1** |

On the weighting before 31 Oct 2026 (`OLD_READINGS=1`), In Step is still first, V8 12th and V9 19th. Common Ground still
leads Even Step, now by 0.15 rather than 0.6, and Next Step by 0.05.

**Into the pack:**
- **Entries.** `regenerate.mjs`'s `SUPPLIED` and `fresh.mjs` (family *Marylebone Balanced*, drawn 7 Nov 2026), both
  shipping into `30-days-off/`. The codes `MB-26-V8` and `MB-26-V9` are new to `links-24/` (as it was) and `links-26/`.
  Their meta files name Balanced V7 as the parent, so page 4 outlines every cell that differs from it.
- **Headlines.** Each says what it has and names no other design.
  - V8: "A 15-minute handover every weekday, from the three 07:30–16:00s to the three 15:45 closers, 19 of 21 weeks on one
    shift time, no fatigue warning in any cover-week placement tested, and 14 days' leave buys 30 days off, as today".
  - V9: the same with "never more than three early or three late weeks in a row outside the cover weeks" in place of the
    weeks on one shift time.
- **The other 22 sheets.** They were re-rendered, and their text is the same but for the date, so the shipped copies stand.
- **The other designs' sheet.** It is now "the other twenty-one designs", with a row for each:
  - V8: the gains V5 and V6 have, no 55-hour week (footnoted) and four weeks off for 13 days' leave, for a larger change of
    start and a weaker weekday fit;
  - V9: V8's gains with shorter runs of earlies or lates, and V8's costs with its two mixed weeks.
  - Claims pin each against the three: what each keeps, and what each pays.

## Balanced V8 and V9 searched again (7 Nov 2026)

**The ask** (owner, 7 Nov 2026): *"Did you say you think you can add 1 more in the score by searching further? If so, do
it. Exhaust the possibilities"*. The thought had been that fewer single rest days and a smaller change of start might
be worth a point at most.

**The answer.**
- **Balanced V9 gained 0.2.** Its weeks 13 and 15 swap their Saturday lates, which brings its heaviest week from 42h 40m
  to 42h 30m: 83.1 to 83.3, still 16th.
- **Balanced V8 gained nothing.** No search found a better layout.
- **Not proven best.** The solver's bounds on these models stay far from its answers, so none of the runs proves that
  nothing better exists.

**What a layout can still move.** With the duties fixed, most of the league's factors are already at the top of their
range for both designs, or are set by the duties:
- **At the top already:** fatigue warnings, the longest run, full weekends, the wait between weekends, one-turn weeks (V8),
  mixed weeks (V8) and the three leave figures.
- **Set by the duties:** the December rules, the train fit, late finishes, long earlies, the closing shift, familiar times
  and contracted days.
- **What is left, at the league's own slopes:**
  - a single rest day, 0.42 each (both have 6; the pack's fewest is 4);
  - a week mixing earlies and lates, 0.40 each (V9 has 2);
  - every rest at 14h 35m or more, 0.33 (both have 14h 20m, and in this table nothing falls between the two);
  - ten minutes off the heaviest week, 0.17;
  - ten minutes off the week-to-week change of start, 0.15 (V8 1h 39m, V9 1h 51m).

**How it was searched.** `exact-rota.py` gained two objectives:
- **`OBJ=league`** weighs those measures at those slopes in one run, so the solver trades them as the league does.
- **`OBJ=iso`** climbs the single rest days from a valid rota, as `OBJ=heavy` and `OBJ=rest` climb theirs.

Every run held each design's floors:
- its duties;
- no 55-hour week in any cover-week placement (`H55=3300`);
- 30 days off and at worst 20 for 14 days' leave;
- four weeks off for 13;
- at most 6 single rest days;
- a 14h 20m shortest rest;
- no week over 42h 55m;
- V9 also its runs of early or late weeks, 3 and 4 with the cover weeks (`BF=3 BW=4`), and at most 3 mixed weeks.

| Search, from the design | V8 | V9 |
|---|---|---|
| The league objective: 20 min (V8), then 20 + 40 min (V9) | no change | heaviest week 42h 30m (42h 40m), then no change |
| Fewest single rest days, 15 min; again letting one-turn weeks fall to 18 (V8) | 6 | 6 |
| Lightest heaviest week, 15 min; again with 18 one-turn weeks (V8) | 42h 30m | 42h 30m |
| No rest under 14h 35m, 10 min | 4 remain | 4 remain |
| Two neighbouring stretches between cover weeks laid again, the other three held: all five pairs, 10 min each | no change | no change |
| Asked outright for 5 single rest days, 15 min | undecided | — |
| Asked outright for no mixed week, or one, V9's runs held, 10 and 15 min | — | undecided |

**The rest days alone.** `tooling/rest-days.py` is new. It is `exact-rota.py`'s rules for which days are worked, with the
times dropped, so what it rules out is ruled out in the full model too.
- **Single rest days.** It found 6 at fewest, and its lower bound never left 0: five is neither found nor ruled out.
  Without the leave rules it finds 4, so the leave rules (four weeks off for 13 above all) are what hold both designs at
  six.
- **V9's mixed weeks.** It found rest days with V9's runs, at most 6 single rest days and no mixed week, and others with
  one. On the no-mixed ones the duties fit only without the 55-hour rule. The result scores 79.9:
  - a 56h 30m week if a cover week falls badly, with 7 days in a row;
  - 14 weeks of 21 on one shift time;
  - a change of start of 3h 19m.
- **The repair.** Started from that rota, the full model could not repair it in 15 min. On the one-mixed rest days the
  duties do not fit even without the 55-hour rule.

So V9's two mixed weeks are what buy its short runs and its other figures together. That is found, not proven.

**What changed in Balanced V9:**
- **Week 13.** Tuesday to Friday 13:30–22:00, its Saturday now the office 14:30–22:30 (was 15:15–23:55): 42h 40m to
  42h 00m.
- **Week 15.** Monday to Thursday 15:45–23:55, its Saturday now 15:15–23:55 (was the office): 40h 40m to 41h 20m.
- **The heaviest week** is now 42h 30m, and the week range 22h 15m–42h 30m.
- **The one cost.** 15:15–23:55 is one of V9's 8 shift times new to learn, so those times now fall in 15 of its 21 working
  weeks, where they fell in 14 (page 2 of its sheet). The league does not count it, since familiar times are counted by
  duty.
- **Unchanged:** the leave, fatigue, runs of 3 and 4, its 2 mixed weeks, 18 of 21 weeks on one shift time, the 1h 51m
  change of start, the 14h 20m shortest rest and every other figure. Its headline still holds.

**The league.** V9 rose from 83.1 to 83.3, still 16th; on the weighting before 31 Oct 2026, from 85.35 to 85.53, still
19th. The heaviest week came down inside every range, so no other score moved.

**Into the pack:**
- **The grid and its sheet.** `results/balanced-v9.json`, and its meta file's seed and lineage. In `regenerate.mjs` the
  fingerprint is `8c679ae2` → `e2b7d945`. The sheet, grid and Import text are re-rendered in `30-days-off/balanced/`;
  the old sheet stays in the history.
- **The summary and the other designs' sheet.** The summary carries V9's heaviest week. The other designs' sheet carries
  its fingerprint, and it also takes up a fix: it had been committed one render behind its own code. Its Balanced
  heading's note read "its subfolder Balanced" where the code says "the folder 4 Proposal sheets/30 days off/Balanced",
  as the other two groups say it. Still one page.
- **Unchanged:** the shortlist sheet, which rebuilt the same pixel for pixel, the read-me and V8.
- **The records.** `30-days-off/README.md`: V9's column and paragraph. `exact-rota.py`'s header: step 10, V9 as now.

**Checked:**
- `FIX=` passes both grids with their own floors.
- `regenerate.mjs --check`: 24 proposals.
- `check.mjs`, `deck-check.mjs`, `leave.mjs --check`, `newlink-data.mjs --check`.
- `pack.py`: 71 files.

## The shortlist redrawn for closing weeks apart (8 Nov 2026)

**The ask.** The owner set a new design target on 8 Nov 2026: "The design target should be no consecutive closing weeks,
with changes between early and late duties placed around useful rest breaks. That captures what colleagues appear to want
much better than simply minimising the average change in start time. Weeks on one time is still Important". The reply
weighed the pack against it and offered three next steps. The owner chose: "Put Level Crossing, Middle Ground and Balanced
V6 as the shortlist for now. Make all the necessary changes including to the app in new links and make them the three
shortlisted read only links in links designer. Do 2 and audit 3, only suggestions for 3." Step 2 was to lay Even Step and
Short Run again with their closing weeks apart. Step 3 was to count the closing weeks and the changes between earlies and
lates on the sheets and in the league, and to drop or reduce the change of start. Both are at the end of this section;
neither changed the pack.

**What a closing week is.**
- A week whose most common Monday-to-Friday duty finishes at the 23:55 close. Two are side by side when they are next to
  each other in the rotation, lapping it.
- A cover week is neither. Its duties are the clerk's to place, and one worked as closes beside a closing week makes two
  in a row on any design, today's included. So the sheets say "cover weeks aside".
- The most common duty can be a tie. Read a second way, three or more of the five weekdays at the close, there are no
  ties, and the two readings agree on every design and on today's link. The shortlist sheet stops if they ever disagree.
- Five designs keep their closing weeks apart: Balanced V5, Balanced V6, Level Crossing, Middle Ground and Second Look.
  So does today's link. Middle Ground alone keeps its 06:20 weeks apart too.

**The shortlist** is Balanced V6, Level Crossing and Middle Ground, A to Z (`tooling/fresh.mjs`: `SHORTLIST`, and
`SHORTLIST_DATE` 8 Nov 2026). Common Ground, Even Step and Short Run left it. All three new ones are in `30-days-off/`,
Balanced V6 in its subfolder `balanced/`. So all three keep today's leave: 14 days' leave buys 30 days off, and four
complete weeks off take 14.

**The sheets.**
- **The shortlist sheet**, rewritten.
  - Page 1 has the lines the three share, the closing weeks kept apart among them; a card for each, with what it does
    best and what it costs against both of the others; and sixteen rows where they differ.
  - Page 2 says why these three, what they cost against today, and how to choose between them.
  - Every comparison is a claim the sheet checks against the grids before it renders.
- **The other designs' sheet** gives the other 21 a row each: what each keeps that the three do not, and what it pays. Its
  lead says which put closing weeks side by side: all but Second Look and Balanced V5.
- **The summary sheet** marks the new three. The ★ now follows the name, which brought the page back above its 80% print
  floor; it renders at 82%.
- **The "What it means if you…" sheet** sets out the new three, A to Z.
- **The proposal sheets are unchanged.** Each compares only with today's link.

**Decided in the change, each the owner's to reverse:**
- **No presentations in the zip, for now.**
  - The eight in `presentations/` are Common Ground's, Even Step's, In Step's and Short Run's, each for colleagues and for
    managers. They were last rebuilt for the shortlist of 5 Nov 2026, and each names it, Common Ground, Even Step and
    Short Run, as the shortlist; In Step's say it is not on it.
  - They stay in the repository, unchanged, and `deck-check.mjs` still holds their 216 rows to the grids.
  - None of the three has a deck yet. `DECKS_KEPT` is empty.
  - `pack.py` refuses a zip that holds a deck while its read-me says none ships.
- **No new measure in the tables or the league.** The closing weeks are a line the three share on the shortlist sheet,
  asserted both ways. Counting them in the league is step 3, audited below and not done.
- **The Links designer's default duty table stays Short Run's** (`links-default-targets.js`). The three staff the same
  number of duties each day at different times: 15 every weekday, 14 on a Saturday and 10 on a Sunday. So the table
  still fits them. Which design's duties the generator starts from is the owner's question, and nothing here answers it.

**The read-me** (`pack.py`) was rewritten for the three.
- With no decks to start from, both audiences start with the sheets. Its history gains a paragraph for 8 Nov.
- Its checks read the shortlist and its date from `fresh.mjs`. It refuses the zip if:
  - a deck is in it, or a shortlisted design has a deck the zip leaves out;
  - `links-proposals.js` no longer says the three were built in at v25.52;
  - the three sheets are no longer in `30-days-off/`, with Balanced V6's in its subfolder.
- **The zip** holds 55 files, where the audit's had 71: the sixteen deck files are out.

**The app, v25.52.**
- **The Links designer.** The built-in, read-only shortlist is Balanced V6, Level Crossing and Middle Ground, A to Z
  (`links-proposals.js`). Each is copied cell for cell from its grid, and `links-proposals.test.mjs` compares each with
  the grid it came from.
- **The New link page.** `newlink-data.js` is regenerated from `tooling/newlink-data.mjs`. The page says what has changed
  since Short Run was shown, and what all three give and cost against today. Its full pack is the three proposal sheets
  and the rules.
- The unit tests and the browser tests follow the new names.

**Step 2: Even Step and Short Run laid again with their closing weeks apart.** This was measured only; nothing is in the
pack.
- Each keeps its duties and rest days, cell for cell, and which weeks are early and which late. Only which week works
  which shift time moved.
- The solver, a scratch copy of `exact-rota.py`, held every other figure at the design's own level or better:
  - the leave;
  - the single rest days;
  - the weekends and the longest wait for one;
  - the runs of early or late weeks;
  - the fatigue warnings;
  - the most hours in seven days.
- It kept the closing weeks apart on a stricter rule than the sheets': no weekday closer at all in two neighbouring weeks.
- Each layout is proved best of its kind, and then the nearest to the design, in cells moved.

| | Even Step | laid again | Short Run | laid again, A | laid again, B |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Fingerprint | `fb7a3988` | `62abf5eb` | `247f3636` | `28e2a503` | `be95ed62` |
| Cells moved | — | 16 | — | 32 | 32 |
| Closing weeks | 13, 14, 22, 26 | 3, 14, 22, 26 | 5, 24, 25, 26 | 5, 14, 22, 24 | 4, 14, 24, 26 |
| Weeks on one shift time | 18 of 21 | 18 of 21 | 18 of 21 | 18 of 21 | 19 of 21 |
| Change of start, week to week | 1h 30m | 1h 34m | 1h 14m | 1h 17m | 1h 27m |
| Most hours in seven days, cover weeks aside | 50h 10m | 50h 10m | 51h 40m | 51h 40m | 51h 10m |
| The same, if a cover week falls badly | 56h 45m | 56h 40m | 56h 50m | 56h 30m | 56h 10m |
| Main league, with today in its ranges | 86.01, 5th | 85.96, 6th of 25 | 82.92, 17th | 82.87, 18th of 25 | 83.11, 17th of 25 |

- **Nothing else moved**: the December rules, the leave, the single rest days, the weekends, the shortest rest, the
  fatigue warnings, the days in a row, the shift times and the fit to the trains. No other design's league score moved.
- **Even Step's re-lay** costs four minutes of change of start, and 18 weeks on one shift time is the most it can have.
  On the sheets' own reading it could have 1h 24m, but only with line 15 closing on the Tuesday, Wednesday and Saturday
  straight after line 14's five, the kind of layout Level Crossing was drawn to avoid. With 17 weeks on one shift time it
  could have 1h 29m, but would score 85.64.
- **Short Run's re-lays.** A costs three minutes of change of start, and nothing frees it, not even the early and late
  weeks laid again. B has 19 weeks on one shift time, the most possible, and 30 minutes fewer in its heaviest seven days.
  It costs 13 minutes of change of start and scores above Short Run.
- **Saturday closers** still sit beside closing weeks in all three, as on Level Crossing.
- **The grids** were in the session's scratchpad, not the repository. Later the same day Even Step's layout and Short Run's
  B joined the pack as Clockface and Short Working (*Clockface and Short Working*, below); Short Run's A did not.
- **The saved duty tables** (`tooling/results/`) keep the Saturday office at 22:00 (*Nine designs deleted*), so the solver
  started from each grid's own table.
- **Checked again** with the pack's own measures: `assess()`, `scoreOrder()`, `leave()`, `kindBlocks()`, `h55Worst()`
  and `league.mjs` with `EXTRA=`. Every figure in the table agreed, and the fingerprints with them.

**Step 3: closing weeks and changes between earlies and lates on the sheets and in the league.** This was audited only;
nothing changed.
- **Where the change of start is used now.**
  - The league: weight 3 of 108, so at most 2.78 points.
  - Page 2 and page 7 (FF18) of every proposal sheet.
  - The summary.
  - The shortlist and other designs' sheets.
  - Even Step's and Common Ground's managers' notes.
  - The read-me.
  - In the app, the Links designer's "Gentle week-to-week change" objective. The New link page shows it nowhere.
- **The closing-week measure.**
  - The most common duty can be a tie, as on Closing Time, Second Look and Balanced V7. Second Look's "none side by
    side" rests on one, which is why the sheets now read it both ways.
  - Pairs are coarse. Middle Ground has none and still 11 closes in some 28 days, where Common Ground and Crossover
    have 18 and 19, Even Step 9 and today's link 8.
  - A cover week worked as closes makes two in a row on every design, so a row would read "0 (up to 2)".
- **The change measure.**
  - Most changes between earlies and lates fall in a cover week, so a row needs "cover weeks aside".
  - Counting the hours off adds nothing, unless the rule depends on the direction of the change. Early to late over one
    rest day gives 42.5 to 46.3 hours off; late to early gives 31.8 to 32.1.
- **The league, eight ways.**
  - With a closing-weeks factor, Level Crossing comes first on every reading tried.
  - The one suggested is the change of start halved to 1.5, closing weeks side by side at 5 and changes without two rest
    days at 3. On it Level Crossing scores 87.4 and Middle Ground 86.0. Common Ground falls to 14th and Crossover to 9th,
    and Balanced V6 stays 23rd.
- **Suggested, in order.**
  1. One shared helper in `report-data.mjs`.
  2. The league as above, with both new factors left out of `OLD_READINGS=1`.
  3. Two rows on page 2 of the proposal sheets.
  4. Both figures in one summary column, in place of the change of start: the page has no room for a fourteenth.
  5. A strap check.
  6. A recount in `check.mjs`.
- **Its recommendation: reduce the change of start, don't drop it.** It is the league's only check on weeks that swap
  between earlies and lates.
- **Its cost.** The shortlist sheet's league claims and several of its comparisons would change, and every sheet that
  shows the league would be re-rendered.

## The shortlist's presentations (8 Nov 2026)

**The ask.** The owner, on 8 Nov 2026: "Write proposals for the shortlist, audit them against existing proposals, then
aesthetic polish and aesthetic consistency check", and later that day: "The presentations once complete should be put in
the new links page on the app". They passed on an outside review too, "only use this as advice".

**How they are built.** `tooling/shortlist-decks.py` writes six decks by rewriting Common Ground's finished pair: a colleague
deck (15 slides) and a managers' deck (10) for each of Balanced V6, Level Crossing and Middle Ground.
- A sentence of Common Ground's is kept only once every figure it quotes is checked equal (`same()`). Everything else is
  rewritten from the grids, and each claim is checked against them first (`check()`).
- It refuses Common Ground's text where it is not true of the design (`CG_ONLY`), another design's name with an ordinary
  space, and "recommend", "union rep" and "fixed rota". It puts the ✓ and ▲ on as it builds, from each figure's colour.
- `deck-check.mjs` reads every row of all fourteen decks back against the grids: 378 agree, none disagree, none unchecked.
- The outside review was used where it is about presenting: the rules as gates, fatigue apart, each design's main cost
  beside its gain, closing weeks said directly, no league position.

**Where they are** (v25.53). In the zip, PowerPoint and PDF, the six and no others: `pack.py` refuses otherwise, and its
read-me routes both audiences through them. The New link page's full-pack card offers the three colleague decks; the
managers' decks stay off it. The eight earlier decks stay in `presentations/`, out of the zip.

**Two audits, and every fix.** Two independent audits read the decks, one the colleague decks and one the managers'. Each
recomputed the figures from the grids with scripts of its own, and both found them right. Their findings were about what
the words claim, and most were about evenness between the three. Each was checked against the grids before it was fixed.
Every fix is in the builder, checked as it builds; the six were rebuilt, their PDFs exported, and the changed slides looked
at in screenshots.
- **One rule for an edge or a cost, for all three.**
  - An edge or a cost against the other two is claimed only where the design is better or worse than both of them by more
    than 5%. `report-data.mjs`'s `fitCmp` calls anything closer "about the same" (`clear_best` in the builder,
    `clearBest` in the shortlist sheet).
  - Before, Middle Ground claimed edges of 2–4% (its Sunday fit, its 06:20 starts, its shortest rest) and Level Crossing a
    2% Saturday cost, while Balanced V6's costs were listed in full.
  - The notes no longer need three gains and three costs each: a design gets as many as it clearly has, one at least.
- **Balanced V6.**
  - Its added edge is the most rest-day breaks of three days or more, about 6 a year (Level Crossing 2, Middle Ground 4).
    It replaces "as many finishes before 14:00 as Level Crossing", which is no edge against both.
  - Its change of start. The notes said its three mixed weeks were why the start moves 4h 08m a week. On the pack's own
    measure the weeks clear of a mixed week average the same; the change comes from switching between early and late
    weeks, the other side of its short runs. The notes now say so.
  - Its weeks on one shift time. "7 of the 21 … as 7 weeks in 16 do today" read as parity. They are said a year now, about
    14 against 18 today, and "all earlies or all lates".
  - Its runs of earlies or of lates were framed as a gain against today ("in return …") and kept off slide 13, "say it if
    asked". They are a cost against today, on its slide 13 card as on the other two.
  - Slide 9's subtitle, "The biggest improvement over today", stood over two ▲ rows. It reads "More weekends off and
    longer rests, but more single rest days".
  - The managers' slide 8 says the worst case beside the gain: "5 at most, not 7; up to 10 if a cover week falls badly,
    today 9".
  - One cost added, the twin of Middle Ground's four at the open: five on duty at 22:00 on a weekday, the rules' minimum,
    where the other two have seven.
- **Level Crossing.**
  - Two costs were not costs against both, and are gone. Its long Monday-to-Saturday shifts and 9-hour Sunday: Balanced V6
    has more, and the same Sunday. The weakest Saturday fit: 17.6 against Balanced V6's 17.3.
  - Its cost now: of the three, the most 06:20 starts and the fewest long breaks, about 2 a year. The slide says "of the
    three", because its 58 starts at 06:20 a year are fewer than today's 62.
  - "Two 06:20–13:45s every weekday" is gone too: Balanced V6 has two finishes before 14:00 every weekday as well. Its
    added edge is the fewest single rest days, about 10 a year, as today.
- **Middle Ground.**
  - "No two 06:20 weeks side by side" is gone from the decks and the shortlist sheet. It held only when week 20, two
    06:20 starts and two 07:00s, was counted a 07:00 week; weeks 17 to 20 all have 06:20 starts.
  - "The longest rest, 14h 35m" was its shortest rest, and within 5% of the others' anyway. It is gone.
  - Its costs now include the Sunday pay. Its Sundays are the shortest, so about £350 a year less premium and overtime pay
    than today, where both others have about £200 less. There are also six on duty at 22:00 on a Saturday, where both have
    seven.
  - Its one possible fatigue warning is no longer a cost against both: Balanced V6 has more.
  - The managers' slide 4 note explaining its weekday fit by today's 11:00–19:30 is gone. No other design's fit was
    explained.
- **All three.**
  - A closing week is defined where the target is first said, in slide 2's notes: one spent mostly on the 23:55 close,
    Monday to Friday. The notes add "as today".
  - Balanced V6 alone has closing weeks either side of a cover week: its weeks 5 and 7, round cover week 6. Its notes say
    that if that week's duties are closes as well it makes three in a row, which is for the roster office to avoid. The
    builder checks the other two have none.
  - FF15 is "more than four starts before 07:00 in a row", not "more than four earlies". The decks' earlies start before
    11:00, and Balanced V6 has five of those in a row in two of its fixed weeks.
  - Today's long shifts are said in full: about 31 duties a year over 8h 30m, Monday to Saturday, not "the Saturday closer
    alone".
  - The managers' slide 1 said the figures "have been checked independently", a sentence from Common Ground's deck. They
    are now worked out "by the same calculations as the proposal sheet".
  - Each design's source is "another design in the pack": Balanced V5, Crossover and Common Ground.
  - The slide 10 notes give the change of start against today for each; Balanced V6's 4h 08m is about the same as today's
    4h 00m.
  - Smaller fixes:
    - a stale pointer to the shortlist sheet's weekday lates;
    - "no shift over 9 hours" said twice;
    - the 13:35 named where a design has none;
    - "(today 26)" written one way;
    - the other two designs named A to Z;
    - "which can avoid it" on Middle Ground's 55-hour note, as on Balanced V6's.

**League language off the sheets.** Both audits found it on the shortlist sheet. It said "Crossover, first in the pack's

bilingual contract who will still have their own links".

    together. When the day is set, it is those two edits.
- **The sheets.** All 24 proposal sheets now say "How this rota would work from January 2027". They were re-rendered with
  `regenerate.mjs`, every fingerprint unchanged. The shortlist, other designs' and "What it means" sheets were re-rendered
  too, because they follow the decks; none of them names the start.

  - Each said 1 January 2027 in five places: the colleagues' title slide, slide 3 and slide 3's notes, the managers' title

## Opening shifts, a gain for most colleagues (8 Nov 2026)

**The ask.** The owner, on 8 Nov 2026: *"Most colleagues prefer the opening shifts, but not all"*, then, of the changes
suggested in reply, *"Make the changes you mentioned"*. The pack had counted a 06:20 start as a cost wherever it judged one,
so for most colleagues it had them the wrong way round.

**What changed.**
- **Level Crossing's card.** The most 06:20 starts of the three, 58 a year each (Balanced V6 50, Middle Ground 48; today
  62), moved from its costs to what it adds, "for those who like them", beside the five at the weekday open they come
  from. Its one cost against both of the others is now the fewest long breaks. The same on page 1 of the shortlist sheet,
  in its "Choose Level Crossing if …" line on page 2, on slide 7 of the managers' deck and in its notes, and in the read-me.
  The claims are unchanged in what they test: the most 06:20 starts clearly, the fewest long breaks clearly.

  what it has.
- **The proposal sheets' page 1** listed "More 06:20 starts" as a concern and "Fewer 06:20 starts" as a gain (`plain.mjs`).
  Now neither: it is a preference, so it stays out of both boxes, as the colleague decks' slide 7 has always kept it
  neutral. The page-1 tile and page 2's row still give the count against today's.
  - Four sheets changed: Clean Slate, Fair Share, Grand Tour and Next Step, each of which listed "More 06:20 starts" as a
    concern. Their fingerprints do not change; only page 1's concerns box does.
  - The other twenty re-rendered identical but for the date, so their shipped copies stand.
- **"What it means if you… prefer earlies"**, on the one-page sheet and on the New link page (v25.56): a row "Starting at
  06:20, the open, a year" (today 62; Balanced V6 50, Level Crossing 58, Middle Ground 48), ranked so more is better for

  - The sheet ran 13px into its footer with the row. A quarter-pixel under each row, a pixel above each band and 3px above
    the notes made the room, 3px to spare; the type and the print floor are as they were.

**Where 06:20 starts stay neutral:** the colleague decks' slide 7, the shortlist sheet's page 2 row "Starting at 06:20,
each a year", and the proposal sheets' tile and page 2. **Not changed:** the eight earlier decks, out of the zip and as built

## The league reweighted for the design target (8 Nov 2026)

**The ask.** The design target the owner set on 8 Nov 2026 (*The shortlist redrawn for closing weeks apart*, above): *"no
consecutive closing weeks, with changes between early and late duties placed around useful rest breaks … Weeks on one
shift time is still Important"*. The league could not see it: it had no measure of closing weeks at all. Step 3 of that
day audited it; an outside review the owner passed on was read as advice (owner: *"only use this as advice"*); the owner
added *"Most colleagues prefer the opening shifts, but not all"*, asked for a *"Deep think … from everything you know"*,
and then, of the changes suggested, *"Make the changes you mentioned"*. The reading below is the main league from that day,
and the one before it is kept, unchanged, as an alternative.

### The method, set before any figure below was read

`league.mjs`'s header says the same; the new measures are `tooling/closing.mjs`, one definition for the league and for any
sheet that prints them.
- **Gates, not points.** The nine December rules, and no more than 219 contracted days a year. Every design passes both,
  so as factors they gave every design the same 25 points of 100 and made every gap look smaller. A design that fails a gate is
  listed after every design that passes, whatever it scores. (No design fails one, so the gates are not exercised today.)
- **Fatigue shown apart.** The worst-case warnings are a column beside the score, as the decks show them, not inside it.
- **Six categories**, each measure 0 to 100 between the worst and the best of the designs, today's link and Familiar Nine,
  as before (`REF_IN_RANGE=1`), weighted and rescaled to 100:

| Category | Measures, weight out of 83 | Share of 100 |
|---|---|---|
| Closing weeks and switches | closing weeks in a row 5; most closes in any 21 days 3; in any 14 days 2; changes between earlies and lates without two rest days, a year 3; finishes at 23:00 or later, a year 2 | 18 |
| Rest and weekends | full weekends off 6; longest wait between them 4; single rest days 4; most days in a row, worst case 3; shortest rest 3 | 24 |
| Leave | 14 days' leave, best stretch 3 and worst 2; leave for four weeks off 2; first days from which 14 days buy 30 off 3 | 12 |
| Earlies and weekly consistency | weeks on one shift time 6; change of start 1.5; opening shifts (06:20 starts), a year 3.5; earlies over 8h 30m Monday to Friday or 9h on a Saturday, a year 4 | 18 |
| Fit to the trains | the fit, the weekday counting five times 11 | 13 |
| Duty lengths and familiar times | familiar shift times, Monday to Saturday 6; the closing shift's length 3; the heaviest week 3 | 14 |

- **What each new measure is** (`closing.mjs`). A closing week has three or more of its five weekdays finishing at 23:00 or
  later: no tie can decide it, where "its most common duty is the close" can be a tie. A cover week is neither, and breaks
  a run. The closes in any 14 and 21 days count every day's closers. A change between earlies and lates is the link's
  own, cover weeks aside, and without two rest days when one or none lies between them.
- **What changed from the league before.**
  - The weeks mixing earlies and lates are gone: they moved with the weeks on one shift time almost exactly, so the two
    counted one property twice.
  - The change of start is halved, not dropped: it is the only measure of how far a start moves between weeks.
  - Opening shifts count as a modest gain, since most colleagues prefer them.
  - A Saturday early counts as long only over 9 hours: the owner's brief for Common Ground allowed 9 at the weekend.
  - Leave is a tenth of the score, from under a twentieth.
- **Close alternatives, not one order.** Each category's weight is moved by a random factor around 1 (log-normal, sigma
  0.4), and each measure's within it (0.3), 4,000 times from a fixed seed; the table gives the place each design reaches or
  beats in 10%, 50% and 90% of those weightings, and the share in which it is first. Designs a point apart swap places
  under small changes of weight, so a band is honest where a single place is not.
- **Why not the outside reviewer's weights as they stand** (six categories at 25/20/20/15/15/5, equal inside each). The
  audit found them lifting Second Look to third with 15 of 21 weeks on one shift time. And leave at a fifth of the score
  splits only the 30-day designs from the 28-day ones: every 30-day design buys 30 days from one first day, as today does
  (Balanced V8 and V9 from two). The reviewer's weights put the same two designs first all the same.

### Balanced V8 laid again with its closing weeks apart (measured only)

**The ask.** The audit found Balanced V5 and V6 keeping their closing weeks apart best of all, and paying for it with 22
single rest days a year and a third of their weeks on one shift time, while Balanced V7, V8 and V9 fix both and put three
closing weeks in a row. The suggestion, which the owner took up: give Balanced V8 what Crossover and Common Ground were given,
the same duties and rest days with the weeks chosen again, and report the cost before anything enters the pack.

**How.** A scratch copy of `exact-rota.py` (its `CLOSE_APART=` and `NEAR=` lines, as for Even Step and Short Run above),
every floor held at Balanced V8's own level: the 55-hour worst case at 51h 00m, the most hours in seven days at 51h 00m, 30
days off and a worst stretch of 20 for 14 days' leave, four weeks off for 13, 6 single rest days in 26 weeks, the heaviest
week 42h 30m and the lightest 24h 00m, a 14h 20m shortest rest, as many backward steps as forward, and FF11 at 9 and 12.
`OBJ=gentle GENTLE_STARTS=15:45 GENTLE_SHARED=0`, then the fewest cells moved among the best layouts.

| | Balanced V8 | Laid again, A | Laid again, C |
|---|---|---|---|
| Fingerprint | `9ac65459` | `e745f804` | `a9e83bf2` |
| What moved | — | which week works which time, 31 cells on 10 lines | the early and late weeks too, 36 cells on 11 lines |
| Closing weeks (in a row, at most) | 2, 3, 4 and 15 (3) | 3, 5, 15 and 23 (1) | 4, 14, 20 and 22 (1) |
| Most closes in any 14 days · any 21 | 11 · 15 | 7 · 9 | 7 · 9 |
| Weeks on one shift time | 19 of 21 | 18 of 21 | 19 of 21 |
| Change of start, week to week | 1h 39m | 2h 01m | 2h 52m |
| Changes between earlies and lates, a year (all at a two-day break) | 6 | 6 | 10 |
| Backward · forward steps (FF17) | 12 · 12 | 10 · 17 | 12 · 12 |
| Most hours in seven days, a cover week placed badly | 51h 00m | 51h 00m | 50h 30m |
| League since 8 Nov, of 25 with the layout beside the pack | 11th of 24, 69.6 | 3rd, 76.6 | 3rd, 76.7 |
| League until 8 Nov, of 25 | 10th of 24, 84.7 | 13th, 83.9 | 15th, 83.6 |

- **Everything else is V8's**: the leave, the single rest days, the weekends, the shortest rest, the heaviest and lightest
  weeks, the 06:20 earlies in a row and no fatigue warning in any cover-week placement tested.
- **Keeping 19 weeks on one shift time with V8's early and late weeks is impossible**: proved infeasible in seconds, on the
  strict reading (no weekday closer at all in two neighbouring weeks) and on the sheets' reading alike. C keeps 19 only by
  moving early and late weeks, for a 2h 52m change of start and ten changes a year.
- **A third layout**, on the sheets' reading at 18 weeks, has a 1h 56m change of start, but two weeks of two closes each
  side by side, so it was not taken.
- **In the league since 8 November either layout stands third**, behind Level Crossing and Middle Ground, from 11th. A is
  in the top three in 61% of the weightings and first in 22%; C in 62% and 24%. In the league before, which cannot see
  closing weeks, either loses about a point.
- **Nothing is in the pack.** The grids are in the session's scratchpad. Later the same day layout A joined the pack as
  Balanced V10 (*Balanced V10*, below); C did not.

**Even Step and Short Run, laid again on 8 November (above), in the same league**, each scored beside the pack alone:
- Even Step's (`62abf5eb`) would be first, 78.3, first in 53% of the weightings and in the top three in 86%. It buys 28
  days off for 14 days' leave, not today's 30.
- Short Run's B (`be95ed62`) would be 4th, 74.5, and A (`28e2a503`) 7th, 74.0.
- As they are, Even Step is 6th and Short Run 17th. None of the three is in the pack.

## Balanced V10 — Balanced V8's weeks laid again with its closing weeks apart (8 Nov 2026)

**The ask.** "Do your suggestion with Balanced v8 to create Balanced v10" (owner, 8 Nov 2026), after *Balanced V8 laid
again with its closing weeks apart*, above, which measured two layouts and put neither in the pack. Layout A, which keeps
Balanced V8's early and late weeks, is Balanced V10 (`MB-26-V10` · `e745f804`). Then: "For now leave the shortlist until I
see Balanced v10". So Balanced V10 is in the pack beside the shortlist, not on it. Nothing on the shortlist changed but one
count: the shortlist sheet's "the other twenty-one" is now "twenty-two".

**What it is.** Balanced V8's duties and rest days, every one, and its early and late weeks. Only which week works which
shift time moved: 31 cells on 10 lines (2, 4, 5, 9, 10, 13, 14, 15, 22 and 23). Its duties are Balanced V7's and Balanced
V6's too. Its closing weeks are 3, 5, 15 and 23 on the sheets' reading (the most common weekday duty a closer) and 3, 15
and 23 on the league's (three or more weekday closes). Never two are together on either reading, nor on the solver's
stricter one (no two neighbouring weeks with any weekday closer).

| | Balanced V8 | Balanced V10 |
|---|---|---|
| Closing weeks in a row, at most | 3 | 1 |
| Most closes in any 14 days · any 21 | 11 · 15 | 7 · 9 |
| Weeks on one shift time | 19 of 21 | 18 of 21 |
| Change of start, week to week | 1h 39m | 2h 01m |
| Backward · forward steps between shifts (FF17) | 12 · 12 | 10 · 17 |
| Main league since 8 Nov, of 25 | 12th, 69.6 | 3rd, 76.6 |
| League until 8 Nov, of 25 | 10th, 84.7 | 13th, 83.9 |

- **Everything else on the sheet is Balanced V8's, figure for figure.** The two sheets' text was compared line by line:
  the December rules, the hard limits, the leave (30 · 23.5 · 20, four weeks off for 13), the single rest days, the
  weekends, the shortest rest, the heaviest and lightest weeks, the shift times, the fit to the trains, and no fatigue
  warning in any cover-week placement tested.
- **19 weeks on one shift time is impossible** with V8's early and late weeks held and no two closing weeks together: the
  solver proves it infeasible, on `CLOSE_APART=2` and on 3 (the sheets' reading).

**How.** `exact-rota.py` gained the two options the measured layouts were drawn with on a scratch copy:
- `CLOSE_APART=`: no two closing weeks side by side. 2 is the strict reading and is the one used here; 1 and 3 are the
  sheets' readings.
- `NEAR=` and `NEAR_CAP=`: the objective is held at its optimum, then the fewest cells moved from a given rota are asked for.

Every objective now goes through one `objective()` call, so `NEAR=` can hold it. The header has the two commands, every
floor at V8's own level. The committed script rebuilds `results/balanced-v10.json` exactly (31 cells, optimal). `FIX=`
finds the grid feasible at the optimum, and refuses Balanced V8's own grid, whose closing weeks 2, 3 and 4 sit together.

**The league.** No other score moved, in either reading: V10 is inside every range both read. So every place below it is
one lower.
- In the main league: Grand Tour 5th, Balanced V8 12th, Common Ground 15th, Balanced V9 16th, Short Openers 17th, Crossover
  19th, Open Return 22nd, Balanced V7 23rd, V6 24th and V5 25th.
- V10 is first in 22% of the 4,000 reweightings and in the top three in 61% (places 1–3–8 at the 10th, 50th and 90th
  percentiles).
- The read-me's league sentence is unchanged: Level Crossing first, by 1.0, and Middle Ground second.

**In the pack.**
- **Its sheet and import file** are in `30-days-off/balanced/`.
- **The summary** has its row. The summary's page margins came down from 6mm to 5mm top and bottom, which prints a 25th
  row at the 80% floor.
- **The other designs' sheet** has its row, against all three shortlisted: has "Balanced V8's leave and no 55-hour week\*,
  with no two closing weeks side by side"; pays "a lighter lightest week, 24h 00m". Claims pin that it is V8's duties and
  rest days laid again with the closing weeks apart, and V8's leave and lightest week. To fit the row, the sheet's cells
  took its own table padding (1.5px above and 2px below, was 2.5 and 3) and its lead lost a few words. The row prints
  17px clear of the footer.
- **The read-me** has a paragraph after the 8 November shortlist's, which ends "It is not on the shortlist". The 22:30
  paragraph names it among the designs drawn with the 22:30 office, and item 4 counts six Balanced designs.
- **The A-to-Z order** puts "Balanced V10" after "Balanced V9", not before "Balanced V5", on the summary, the other designs'
  sheet and the folder lists (`localeCompare` with `numeric`).

**Its presentations.** A colleague deck and a managers' deck, drawn by `shortlist-decks.py` as the three shortlisted were,
from Common Ground's finished pair, and set beside the shortlist (`BESIDE`):
- **Duty lines.** Its shift times, closers and new times are Balanced V6's, since its duties are V6's (`DUTIES`).
- **Its own lines.** Its weeks, single rest days, trade-off cards and leave are its own. Four weeks off for 13 days'
  leave, a day less than today, is said wherever "as today" stood.
- **The managers' title slide** reads "A new CEA link drawn after the shortlist".
- **The managers' "Why" slide** says what it shares with all three. It adds, against all three, four weeks off for 13
  days' leave (5% clear). It costs, against Level Crossing and Middle Ground, whose duties differ, a weaker weekday fit
  (V6's 40.0). Against Balanced V6 its shifts can cost nothing, so its notes say what its weeks change against V6's both
  ways, every figure checked.
- **Checks.** `deck-check.mjs` holds both decks' tables to the grid: 432 rows across all sixteen decks agree. Rebuilt with
  the changed builder, the shortlist's six decks match the committed ones part for part. Only the chart workbook's save
  time differed, and the committed files were kept.
- **Not in the zip.** The zip carries the shortlist's decks only, so V10's are not in it.

**Checks.** `regenerate.mjs --check` (25 proposals), `check.mjs`, `closing.mjs --check`, `leave.mjs --check`,
`newlink-data.mjs --check` (the app's data is unchanged, so no version bump), `deck-check.mjs` and `pack.py` (69 files).

*Even Step was discarded on 9 Nov 2026 (Four designs discarded, below). Clockface is unchanged; its row on the sheet of the

closing weeks apart and put neither in the pack. The owner then named them, "Clockface, Short Working", taking Short Run's
layout B, and asked for "the presentations for non shortlisted designs where they exist, to match the updated designs". With
"For now leave the shortlist until I see Balanced v10", both are in the pack beside the shortlist, not on it, as Balanced V10 is.

**What they are.**

- Both are in `proposals/`, beside their parents, since neither buys 30 days off for 14 days' leave. Each code keeps its
  parent's table, T5 and F1. Short Working's is `SWK`, not `SW`: `SW-26-F1` was Second Wind's (below).

| | Even Step | Clockface | Short Run | Short Working |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Closing weeks in a row, at most | 2 | 1 | 3 | 1 |
| Most closes in any 14 days · any 21 | 9 · 9 | 6 · 6 | 9 · 11 | 6 · 8 |
| Weeks on one shift time | 18 of 21 | 18 of 21 | 18 of 21 | 19 of 21 |

| Main league since 8 Nov, of 27 | 9th, 74.0 | 1st, 78.3 | 20th, 67.7 | 6th, 74.5 |
| League until 8 Nov, of 27 | 5th, 86.0 | 6th, 86.0 | 20th, 82.9 | 19th, 83.1 |

- **Everything else on each sheet is its parent's, figure for figure.** Each pair of sheets was compared line by line: the
  December rules, the hard limits, the leave, the single rest days, the weekends, the runs, the shortest rest, the heaviest
  and lightest weeks, the shift times, the fit to the trains, and the fatigue warnings, on the same checks.
- **The most each can have.** With Even Step's weeks held and no two closing weeks together, 19 weeks on one shift time is
  infeasible, so Clockface's 18 is the most. With Short Run's weeks held at its floors, 20 is infeasible with the
  closing-weeks rule and without it, so Short Working's 19 is the most.

**How.**
- The committed `exact-rota.py` rebuilds both grids exactly, with the commands in its header. First the best layout at the
  parent's own floors with `CLOSE_APART=2`: optimal at 330110 and 327825. Then `NEAR=` the parent for the fewest cells moved
  at that optimum: 16 and 32, optimal, and the same grid on a second run.
- Each is laid on its parent's own duties as its grid has had them since the Saturday office closed at 22:30
  (`results/clockface-table.json`, `results/short-working-table.json`, counted from the parents' grids). The saved tables
  Even Step and Short Run were drawn from keep the office at 22:00.
- `FIX=` finds each grid feasible at the optimum and refuses each parent's: Even Step's closing weeks 13 and 14 sit together,
  as do Short Run's 24, 25 and 26.

**A code names one design, for good.** A code is what goes on a printout, so `regenerate.mjs` now refuses an entry that takes
a code once given to a design that was withdrawn, deleted or renamed (`RETIRED_CODES`, fifteen of the pack's own and the
outside reviewer's three), and two entries that share a code. Mutated with `scripts/mutate.mjs`: Short Working given
`SW-26-F1`, and Clockface given Even Step's code, each fail with their own message. `pack.py` read `fresh.mjs`'s dates with a
two-letter pattern, which would have missed `SWK-26-F1`; it reads two or three letters now.

**The league.**
- No other score moved in either league: both are inside every range both read. So every place below each is one lower.
- **In the main league Clockface is first, 78.3**, 0.5 ahead of Level Crossing. It is first in 51% of the 4,000
  reweightings and in the top three in 75% (places 1–1–5 at the 10th, 50th and 90th percentiles). Level Crossing is 2nd,
  Middle Ground 3rd, Balanced V10 4th, Next Step 5th and Short Working 6th, 74.5.
- In the league until 8 Nov, which cannot see the closing weeks, Clockface is 6th, 86.0, just behind Even Step's 86.01, and
  Short Working 19th, 83.1.
- The read-me's league sentence is read from `league.mjs`, so it now names Clockface first, by 0.5 points, and Level
  Crossing second.

**In the pack.**
- **Their sheets and import files** are in `proposals/`.
- **The summary** has their rows: 27, on one sheet at the 80% floor. That needed 40px. The dense page now sets the notes under
  each figure ("up to 3§", "blocks 5 (up to 7)") at a line height of 1; the band's sentence on a design that breaks a hard
  limit prints only when one does; and "Week-to-week" is kept whole, so no heading runs to a fourth line. The type and the
  padding are as they were.
- **The other designs' sheet** is two pages now, split at the folders: twenty-three rows ran 94px past the footer of one.
  Page 1 has the band, the lead and the proposals folder's fourteen; page 2, under a continued band, the 30-day folder's
  designs and the Balanced ones. The footnote on "no 55-hour week" is on each page with a row that uses it.
  - Clockface has Even Step's early times, every one of today's, and its steadier weekly hours, with no two closing weeks
    side by side, and pays with fewer early finishes.
  - Short Working has Short Run's shorter weekday lates and closer weekday fit, and 19 of 21 weeks on one shift time, more
    than any of the three, and pays with nine-hour earlies, 20 a year each.
  - Even Step's and Short Run's rows now say Clockface and Short Working are their weeks laid again, as Common Ground's and
    Crossover's name Middle Ground and Level Crossing.
  - Claims pin each to its parent's duties and rest days with the closing weeks apart on both readings, what each keeps and
    pays against the three, and the figures each row leans on.
- **The shortlist sheet** changed only in its count: "the other twenty-four", from "twenty-two". The shortlist is as it was.
- **The read-me** (`pack.py`):
  - its paragraph after the shortlist's covers all three late designs and ends "None of the three is on the shortlist";
  - the 22:30 paragraph names both among the designs drawn since;
  - it says "a two-page sheet", reading the page count from the PDF, where it had said one page in two places.
- **The zip** holds 73 files, from 69: the two sheets and their import files.

**Their presentations.** A colleague deck and a managers' deck each, in `presentations/` as PowerPoint and PDF, set beside
the shortlist as Balanced V10's are. Like V10's they are not in the zip, which carries the shortlist's decks only.
- **Drawn from the parents' finished decks** by `tooling/relay-decks.py`. Each design has its parent's duties, rest days,
  cover weeks and early and late weeks, so every sentence of Even Step's and Short Run's decks stays true of it except three

  - **the figures that moved:** the busiest seven days a badly placed cover week can make (56h 40m and 56h 10m), the change
    of start week to week (1h 34m and 1h 27m), and Short Working's 19 of 21 weeks on one shift time;
  - **the closing weeks:** the colleague bar says "never two closing weeks in a row, cover weeks aside", and the notes on the
    short version, the rest slide and the strip say what a closing week is and that none follows another, as today;
  - **the old shortlist** (Common Ground, Even Step, Short Run): the managers' cover reads "A new CEA link drawn after the
    shortlist", the fit notes set each beside Balanced V6, Level Crossing and Middle Ground, and the "Why" slide says what
    it shares with all three and what it adds and costs against all three, by the 5% rule the shortlist's decks use.
- **What each adds and costs, against all three.**
  - Clockface adds every early Monday to Saturday a time worked today, the fewest new shift times (five) and the fewest
    single rest days. It costs four weeks off for 15 days' leave, not 14, and 28 days off at best, not 30; the fewest
    finishes before 14:00; and no rest-day break of three days or more.

    weeks (19 of 21 on one shift time, a 1h 27m change of start, the fewest single rest days). It costs the 9-hour weekday
    07:00–16:00, about 20 a year each; the same leave; and no finish before 14:00 and no break of three days or more.

    other figure is the parent's.

- **Slide 10's strip** is the parent's picture, pixel for pixel. It draws rest days, cover weeks, full weekends and which

  sheet". Nothing else changed in V10's colleague deck; its managers' deck rebuilt identical bar the chart workbook's save

- **Checks.** `deck-check.mjs` reads both new pairs: 540 rows across all twenty decks agree, from 432. Mutated with

**What stays, and why.**
- **Short Openers' grid**, `results/short-openers.json` with its meta, out of the pack. Crossover's and Balanced V8's weeks
  were laid from its rest days, and `exact-rota.py`'s recipe for Balanced V8 starts from it: it named the shipped copy in
  `30-days-off/`, and names this one now, cell for cell the same. The meta says why it stays.
- **Even Keel's shift times**, written into `tooling/exact/common.py` as `EVEN_KEEL`. The exact solver lets a repair use the
  times of the first shortlist's designs, read from their grids; written there, the times are as they were — the same 29 for
  Short Run and 30 for Clockface, checked against a list built from the deleted grid.
- **`tooling/in-step-decks.py`**, and the deck builders before Common Ground's that read In Step's grid or figures
  (`short-run-decks.py`, `even-step-decks.py`, `deck-polish.py`). They are the record of how Short Run's, Even Step's and
  Common Ground's presentations were made, and none of them could finish a deck after the shortlist of 8 Nov 2026 in any
  case: the polish refuses. The two builders of the decks in use, `shortlist-decks.py` and `relay-decks.py`, take only
  helpers from the polish; both were run after the deletion, and their twelve decks came out identical to the committed ones
  but for the chart workbook's save time, so the committed files were kept.
- **Their codes**, in `regenerate.mjs` → `RETIRED_CODES`: a code names one design for good, so none of the five can be given
  to another design (a mutation giving Clockface `IS-26-T4` fails the check, naming In Step).
- **`links-proposals.js`** in the app names Even Keel and In Step in its history comments: true of their day, and changing a
  served file would start a Hosting deploy for nothing anyone sees. **The app does not change**, and there is no version
  bump.
- **This README's sections** about them, each marked deleted with a note under its heading, the 30-day folder's README,
  which says the same for Open Return and Short Openers, and the tooling's comments that name them as history.

**The league.** Unlike Both Ways and Fine Tune, three of the five set an end of four of the ranges both leagues read
(today's link and Familiar Nine inside them, as the main table reads), so every other score moved:

| Measure | Range before | Range after | The end set by |
|---|---|---|---|
| Days off at best for 14 days' leave | 27 to 30 | 28 to 30 | Second Look |
| Long earlies, a year each | 0 to 40 | 0 to 21.7 (the league before 8 Nov: 0 to 32.5) | In Step |
| Closing shift, Monday to Saturday average | 7h 45m to 8h 45m | 7h 55m to 8h 45m | Open Return |
| Heaviest week | 41h 05m to 43h 50m | 41h 45m to 43h 50m | In Step |

The designs with 20 long earlies a year — Short Working, Short Run, Second Edition and Long Break — fell furthest: with In

**The pack.** 22 designs: eleven in `proposals/` and eleven in `30-days-off/`, six of them in `balanced/`. The zip holds 63
files, from 73: the five sheets and their five import files (In Step's presentations were not in the zip).

- **Unchanged:** the 22 proposal sheets (rendered again, text identical but the date, so the committed files were kept), the

- **`RULES.md`** (in the zip as the technical notes) gives the app's release history without naming In Step: "v25.02
