Short Run and the made-up weeks), and long earlies counted over 9 hours on a weekday (Short Run's 20 read 0). And on the
tree: `regenerate.mjs --check`, 22 proposals; `check.mjs`, everything agrees; `leave.mjs --check` and `newlink-data.mjs
--check`, ok; `deck-check.mjs`, 486 rows agree, from 540 (In Step's two decks); `pack.py`, 63 files; `npm test`, 2,561 pass.

**Getting back.** `git checkout 69ebb70f -- <path>` restores any of the files from main (`93530a9b` on this branch). To put
a design back in the pack: its files, its `SUPPLIED` entry, its strap, family and date in `fresh.mjs`, its row and claims on
the other designs' sheet, and its name out of `pack.py`'s `DELETED`. It may have its own code back; `RETIRED_CODES` refuses
only a code given to another design.

**Outside the repository.** Zips sent before this change hold the five; the one sent with it does not.

## The whole pack polished from screenshots (9 Nov 2026)

The owner asked for an aesthetic polish and consistency pass over the whole pack, from screenshots, checking every
surface. Every page of every sheet, every slide of the six shortlisted decks (and Balanced V10's two), the read-me and the
import files were rendered to images and read by seven independent reviews. Each finding was checked against its crop
before anything changed, and each was fixed in the tooling or declined with a reason. No rota changed: every fingerprint
is as it was, and no figure moved.

**The proposal sheets.**
- **Dates.** The sheets printed "Prepared 8 Nov 2026": `longDates` (`link.mjs`) matched a typed space but not the
  `&nbsp;` the plain edition writes. It now reads both, and refuses a sheet that still holds a short date.
- **Page 1 fits by steps, with a floor.** Balanced V8's and V10's staff boxes ended on the footer rule. The page now
  tightens in three steps only when it must, gives back what it does not need, and is refused if it ends less than 8px above
  its footer (`render.mjs`).
- **Keep-together rules**, so a figure keeps what it belongs to: "about 12", "30 days off", "(today 7)", "up to 5 (today
  7)", "218.6 contracted days a year", "December 2026", "day-on-day-off", "a perfect match" and a dozen more. A cell, list
  item or paragraph no longer ends on one short word. Tile 4's triples stack, one per line.
- **Colour says one thing.** Balanced V5's "Partly" (the train fit closer on some days, none worse) was green like "Yes";
  it is unshaded. Page 7's present marks were mixed with black, darker than the body text; they wear the key's amber now,
  and a present cell is tinted as page 2 tints the cells it asks a reader to look at. Page 6 gained a key: ✓ met · ✕ not
  met, against the rule rather than against today. On page 3, "one-turn weeks" lost the cover weeks' olive.
- **Alignment.** Page 5's score box hangs its ✓, so the proposal's figures sit under today's. Page 4's shift-time table fits
  its column on every sheet (it ran 12px into the side card). Page 5's weekday heat map is set in the Saturday and Sunday
  tables' type; it was 7 to 13% smaller, with room to spare on every sheet.
- **Pages 7 and 8.** Page 7's notes are in two columns, as page 5's are. Page 8's three box headings are all navy (only
  "Firm" was green), their lists start level, and a hairline sets the closing note apart from the column above it.
- **Words.** Page 6's column says "Proposed", as page 7's does; page 4's cards say "Early" and "Late", as its table does;
  *Managing rail staff fatigue* is in italics, as *Fatigue Factors* is; "multiplied by 10,000. 0 would be" (which read as
  10,000.0) became "multiplied by 10,000. Lower is closer; 0 would be a perfect match."

**The summary, shortlist, other designs, what-it-means and rules sheets.**
- **The best of three is bold navy, not green.** Green says better than today everywhere else in the pack, and Balanced
  V6's "3 · 5" wore it beside today's 2 · 3. The summary's ★ is navy too, and its footnote now names its one colour.
- **Spacing and breaks.** The summary's table and the other designs' lead sat 1mm under their mastheads; the shortlist's
  ✓ list hangs its lines; "7 of 21 / weeks", "Level / Crossing", "side / by side", "three- / and four-day" and "8- / hour"
  no longer split; the rules sheet's run-in notes have one space between them, not what read as two.
- **The summary's §** is raised, as its † and * are. The rules PDF is titled "The December 2026 rules — the new CEA link",
  as its siblings are titled.
- **`RULES.md`** gives every month in full, writes "35h", and marks its changed rules "CHANGED" without the ▲, which means
  worse than today everywhere else.

**The decks** (`shortlist-decks.py`; all eight rebuilt, `deck-check.mjs` 486 rows agree).
- **The managers' slide 7.** "This one adds" wore a green ✓ and "This one costs" an amber ▲, the pack's marks for better and
  worse than today, though both compare the design with the other two: Middle Ground's cost line wore a ▲ for "four at the
  open every day", which slide 3 ticks as better than today. They are a navy + and − now. The shared lines keep their ✓.
- **Marks a projector can show.** Slide 7's marks are 15pt in their circles (12 before) and slide 9's ticks 18pt (15).
- **One way of writing a shift's length:** "no shift over 9h 00m" and "09:00–18:00, 9h 00m", as slide 6 and the sheets.
- **Breaks:** "on weekdays and Saturdays" (Middle Ground's slide 8) and "a 12:00 late" (Balanced V6's and V10's slide 13)
  move down whole.
- **The files say who made them.** Every deck's properties gave its author, last editor and company as "PptxGenJS", the
  library the first deck was drawn with, and the exported PDFs carried the same author; they now say Marylebone Roster —
  Links designer. The dates are kept.

**The read-me.** The presentations are listed A to Z, as it says they are (they followed the folders' order). The
shortlist's paragraphs and list have blank lines between them, the league's reweighting is a paragraph of its own, and no
paragraph ends on one word alone.

**Declined, with the reason:**
- page 2's closing-shift row stays a little smaller, and a "·" may end a line: the pack's 1 October convention, which the
  decks' slide 14 follows too (a wider column was tried there: the line is 2.85in as set, and broke inside "8h 10m");
- page 4's narrow shape card keeps "Today", as page 2's column does; "Today's link" wraps there;
- the what-it-means sheet marks a row's best on the fixed duties' figure, so a tie there is a tie: the New link page reads
  the same rule, and changing it would be a change to the app;
- the read-me's "5 Import files for the Links page" keeps a line of its own: every label is its folder's full name (the
  8 November audit), and the import files stay machine-read text;
- a deck table cell cannot hang its mark, so the decks' figure columns stay centred with their marks;
- the decks' secondary grey (5.0:1 on the pale tiles, which passes AA) and slide 10's picture margin stay;
- page 7 keeps its own key against the guidance; page 1 keeps its breathing space; Clean Slate keeps its five-wide cards;
  the cover meta line and closing sign-off keep their different jobs.

## Four designs discarded (9 Nov 2026)

**The ask** (owner, 9 Nov 2026), after asking which proposals could be discarded now, the Balanced series aside, and then
adding Balanced V5: *"Discard those three and balanced v5 now please, very carefully"*. The three were Common Ground,
Crossover and Even Step. Each has a design in the pack drawn from it — Middle Ground, Level Crossing and Clockface — with
the same duties and rest days laid in new weeks so that no two closing weeks come together. On the main league each of
those is level with it on rest, leave, fit and duties, ahead on the closing weeks by 4.3 to 10.9 points and behind on the
earlies by 0.04 to 0.89, and 4.2 to 10.2 points ahead in all. Balanced V5 was last on both leagues; Balanced V6 is its
weeks with a handover. The choice is the owner's; none of the four was on the shortlist of 8 Nov.

| Design | Code · fingerprint | What it was | Main league before, of 22 | Files |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Common Ground | `CG-26-T8` · `03af96b2` | today's link taken to 26 weeks on the owner's brief, 4 Nov 2026, in `30-days-off/`; on the shortlist 5 to 8 Nov | 12th, 69.7 | its sheet, import file and JSON deleted, and its two presentations' PDFs; its two PowerPoints moved to `tooling/deck-template/` |
| Crossover | `CO-26-T10` · `6596c0d9` | two 13:45s every weekday and a handover to the three 15:45 closers, 7 Nov 2026, in `30-days-off/` (drawn as Safe Hands) | 17th, 68.5 | its sheet, import file and JSON deleted |
| Even Step | `ES-26-T5` · `fb7a3988` | In Step's weeks with nothing over 8h 30m Monday to Saturday, 31 Oct 2026, in `proposals/`; on the shortlist 31 Oct to 8 Nov, recommended until 5 Nov | 8th, 74.0 | its sheet, import file and JSON deleted, and its two presentations' PDFs; its two PowerPoints moved to `tooling/deck-template/` |
| Balanced V5 | `MB-26-V5` · `ded4a15f` | a one-page roster drawn up outside the app, 5 Nov 2026, in `30-days-off/balanced/`: weeks of 06:20 starts and weeks of 08:00 starts | 22nd, 48.4 | its sheet, import file and JSON deleted |

Sixteen tracked files deleted, and four moved byte for byte. In the league as weighted until 8 Nov they were 1st (Crossover,
87.5), 3rd (Common Ground, 86.9), 5th (Even Step, 86.6) and 22nd (Balanced V5, 53.7) of 22.

**What stays, and why.**
- **Two bases** — new, `fresh.mjs` → `BASES`: designs out of the pack that a deck builder still starts from by name.
  `shortlist-decks.py` builds the shortlist's and Balanced V10's presentations from Common Ground's finished decks, and
  `relay-decks.py` builds Clockface's from Even Step's. Their four PowerPoints moved, unchanged, to `tooling/deck-template/`,
  beside Second Nature's, and their grids are `tooling/common-ground.json` and `tooling/even-step.json`, with their duty
  tables in `results/`. `regenerate.mjs --check` holds each base: its grid's fingerprint, its code retired as its own, and
  never an entry in `SUPPLIED`, so a base is never rendered, shipped, scored or listed on a sheet. `deck-polish.py` reads the
  bases' codes with the pack's, and `deck-check.mjs --print` gives their figures, which the builders hold every kept
  sentence to. The folder was ignored by git as a whole, for Second Nature's decks, which are built there; it now ignores
  those by name, so a base in it is tracked like any other file and one added later cannot be left out of a commit.
- **Crossover's grid**, `tooling/crossover.json`, and **Balanced V5's**, `tooling/results/balanced-v5.json`, each with its
  meta, out of the pack. Level Crossing is Crossover's duties and rest days in new weeks, and `closing.mjs` pins four closing
  weeks in a row on Crossover's and Common Ground's grids; Balanced V6 is Balanced V5's weeks with a handover, and V7 to V10
  keep V6's duties. Each meta says why it stays (`kept`). Crossover's duty table is Level Crossing's (T10), Common Ground's
  Middle Ground's (T8).
- **Short Run's presentations**, in `presentations/` and out of the zip: made for the shortlist of 5 Nov, they name Common
  Ground and Even Step as its other two, true of their day, and they are where `relay-decks.py` starts Short Working's.
- **Their codes**, in `regenerate.mjs` → `RETIRED_CODES`, so none can be given to another design.
- **The deck builders before the shortlist's** (`common-ground-decks.py`, `even-step-decks.py`, `short-run-decks.py`,
  `in-step-decks.py`) and the tooling's comments that name the four: the record of how things were made.
- **The app**, which names Common Ground and Even Step only in history comments (`links-proposals.js`, two stylesheets and
  two tests) and in a made-up design name in one test: true of their day, and a change to a served file would start a
  Hosting deploy for nothing anyone sees. **The app does not change**, and there is no version bump.
- **This README's sections** about them, each marked discarded with a note under its heading, and notes under those of
  Middle Ground, Relief Point, Level Crossing and Clockface; the 30-day folder's README says the same for the three from it.

**The league.** In the league as weighted until 8 Nov (`LEAGUE=31oct`) none of the four set an end of any range it reads,
so no score moved, and every place below each of them rose. In the main league (`REF_IN_RANGE=1 node league.mjs`) two
ranges changed, both closing-weeks measures where Crossover set the far end:

| Measure | Range before | Range after | The end set by |
|---|---|---|---|
| Closing weeks in a row, at most | 1 to 4 | 1 to 3 | Crossover and Common Ground |
| Closes in any 21 days, at most | 6 to 16 | 6 to 15 | Crossover |

Even Step's decks); `pack.py`, 55 files; `npm test`, 2,561 pass. Mutated with `scripts/mutate.mjs`, each check fails as it
should: a base's fingerprint changed, and its code retired under another name (`regenerate.mjs --check`); a read-me sentence
naming Crossover, Clockface's closer clause, and Short Run's decks shipped (`pack.py`); a deck note naming Crossover

## Balanced V10's Sundays in its 30 days off (9 Nov 2026)

**The ask.** "Balanced v10 is great, but there are a lot of Sundays in the 14 days AL for 30 days off period. Is that
avoidable?", then "If it is do a v11 … reducing the amount of lost sunday rostered overtime in that period would be
preferable, but only if possible" (owner, 9 Nov 2026). **No Balanced V11 was made:** nothing found keeps everything Balanced
V10 has. Nothing in the pack changed.

**What V10 gives up.** A Sunday duty is overtime, so it costs no leave. A stretch that 14 days' leave buys therefore runs
through the rostered Sundays inside it, and the person gives each one up. V10 has two 30-day stretches, from week 4's
Saturday and from week 5's Sunday. Each holds five Sundays: three rostered duties, one rest day and one cover-week Sunday.
Today's link has one, from week 14's Tuesday, with four Sundays: two rostered, one rest day, one cover-week Sunday.

| Days off that 14 days' leave buys, giving up | any number of Sundays | 2 at most | 1 at most | none |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Today's link | 30 | 30 | 28 | 25 |
| Balanced V10 | 30 | 29 | 28 | 25 |

So on V10 someone who keeps a third Sunday has 29 days off, not 30. Giving up one Sunday or none, V10 matches today.

**Why: four weeks off for 13 days.** V10's four weeks off, Sunday to Saturday, cost 13 days' leave, a day less than today's
14, and that day is paid for with the same Sundays.
- A cover week costs four days' leave. A working week costs at least three, and exactly three only when it has four duties
  and one of them is the Sunday.
- So four weeks for 13 are a cover week and three such weeks, which means three rostered Sundays.
- The 30-day stretches around those four weeks give up all three.

The solver proves it on the leave rules alone: `LV_FOUR=13` with `SUN_ALL=2` is infeasible in 32 s.

**What was tried for a V11.** The aim: keep V10's four weeks for 13, and add a second 30-day stretch that gives up two
Sundays at most, with every other figure at V10's own level.
- **On the leave rules alone it exists.** With weeks 2–10 laid again on V10's other weeks, two Sundays is reached and proved
  the least there.
- **With every V10 floor, none was found.**
  - Week 9 freed together with any one other week: infeasible for all 20 other weeks, both with V10's 6 single rest days and
    with 7. That is 40 runs, each settled in seconds.
  - Weeks 2–10 laid again: it needs 8 single rest days, or V10's two caps on the hours in seven days (51 hours) both lifted
    past 53. With 8 single rest days, 18 weeks on one shift time was not found in 5 minutes.
  - The whole rota, starting from V10: nothing in 15 minutes. With only the leave, single-rest-day and hours rules: nothing
    in 7, and not proved impossible either.
- **Giving up four weeks for 13**, back to 14 as today, does not obviously help. On the leave rules alone, every 30-day
  stretch could then give up one Sunday at most. With V10's other floors, nothing was found: 15 minutes on the whole rota
  from V10, and two 3-minute runs on weeks 2–10 and 4–13.

**The commands.** Run from `tooling/`. F is Balanced V10's floors, as in `exact-rota.py`'s header; LEAVE keeps the leave rules
and loosens the rest:
```
F="H55=3060 H7=3060 LV_BEST=30 BEST_LEN=30 LV_FOUR=13 LV_WORST=20 FF11_FIXED=9 FF11_WORST=12 ISO=6 HEAVY=2550 LIGHT=1440 REST=860 GAP=5 FF17=0 ONE_MIN=18 CLOSE_APART=2"
LEAVE="LV_BEST=30 BEST_LEN=30 LV_FOUR=13 LV_WORST=20 REST=0 HEAVY=9999 H7=9999 FF11_FIXED=30 FF11_WORST=30 ISO=99 GAP=26 FF17=99 BLOCK=21"
env $F FIX=results/balanced-v10.json OBJ=sunmax python3 exact-rota.py results/balanced-v7-table.json out.json   # most 3, fewest 3
env $LEAVE SUN_ALL=2 OBJ=sunmax python3 exact-rota.py results/balanced-v7-table.json out.json 240                # infeasible
env $F SKELETON=results/balanced-v10.json SKELETON_FREE=9,2 SUN_SOME=2 OBJ=sunmin python3 exact-rota.py results/balanced-v7-table.json out.json 60
```
The last is infeasible, and so is every other line in place of 2. `exact-rota.py` gained `SUN_ALL=`, `SUN_SOME=` and
`OBJ=sunmax|sunmin` for this. They are built only when asked for, so every recorded command keeps its model: V10's own
`FIX=` check is still optimal at 338585. `FIX=` with `SUN_SOME=2` refuses V10's grid. A mutation that counts cover-week
Sundays instead of rostered ones makes it accept the grid, which shows the count is the rostered Sundays.

## Notes for the whole set

**Full weekends off, per year (owner, 2 Oct 2026).** A longer rotation's larger count is not more weekends in a year, so
"7 in 26" against "4 in 20" made the reader divide. The sheets now say it per person per year, as late finishes and 06:20
starts already were:
- **page 1:** "About 14 full weekends off a year (today 10), 7 in 26 weeks, …";
- **page 2:** a "Full weekends off" row in *Each person's year, on average*, with the share of weeks (27%, today 20%);
  the rotation figure stays, relabelled "Full weekends off, in the rotation";
- **the summary:** under the count.

The weighted league scores weekends as a share of weeks for the same reason. The 24-line sheets gained the same per-year line the same day, their one change since that folder was frozen
(`../links-24/README.md`).

**Everything in one download:** `links-26-proposals.zip`, built on demand by `tooling/pack.py` and **not committed**
(owner, 3 Nov 2026 — each committed copy added ~15 MB to the repository's history, sixteen times by then; the
repository ignores `*.zip`, so run the script and send the file). It is laid out as the 24-line pack
(`../links-24/links-24-proposals.zip`) was:
1. a *Read me first* note;
2. the one-page summary, the two-page shortlist sheet and the one-page "What it means if you…" sheet;
3. the rules;
4. the presentations of the shortlisted designs (Balanced V6's, Level Crossing's and Middle Ground's since 8 Nov 2026: *The shortlist's presentations*);
5. every proposal sheet;
6. the import files;
7. the technical notes: `RULES.md`. This README was in them until 5 Nov 2026, and stays in the repository (owner: "Leave
   it out of the pack").

`tooling/pack.py` builds it from this folder, every design in `regenerate.mjs`'s `SUPPLIED` list included, and stops
if a file is missing. The 24-line pack was zipped by hand, so it went stale after a re-render; this one is rebuilt by
running `python3 docs/links-26/tooling/pack.py` after any re-render.

**26 in the app since v24.47 (3 Oct 2026).** `ROTATING_LINES = 26` in `links-design.js`: the Links page lays out 26
lines, so the import files in the pack paste straight in and the page re-runs every check on them; the shortlisted
designs are built into that page as examples (three from v24.47, Closing Time from v25.01, In Step and Short Run from v25.02, Even Step, In Step and Short Run from v25.04, Common Ground, Even Step and Short Run from v25.36, and from v25.52 the three on the shortlist now, A to Z), copied cell for cell from `30-days-off/`, Balanced V6's from its subfolder `balanced/` (`links-proposals.js` says so, and
says to re-copy if one changes). The tooling still
passes 26 to every app function explicitly — harmless now, and it keeps the sheets' line count stated in one place
(`tooling/link.mjs`) rather than inherited. Until that release the page was on 24 lines and refused the files; the
read-me said so, and now says the opposite.
